Stockport County avoid transfer emergency before Reading FC game after international u-turn

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Stockport County have reached an agreement with England for goalkeeper Ollie Whatmuff to remain at the club, which means he can play against Reading on Saturday.

It has been a positive season for the League One outfit so far, as they currently sit second in the third tier, whilst they came from two behind to beat Cray Wanderers in extra time to book their place in the second round of the FA Cup.

However, that victory came at a cost, as Stockport saw goalkeeper Luca Ashby-Hammond sent off in the first half against the non-league side.

Whilst Ashby-Hammond has been backup to Whatmuff for the majority of the campaign, the on-loan Man City youngster had been called up by England’s under-20's side over the international break.

So, a suspension to Ashby-Hammond left Jimmy McNulty in a very difficult position, as the Stockport chief would be without his two goalkeepers for the game against Reading.

As a result, it was revealed the club would hold talks with Darren Moore about the possibility of Whatmuff not joining up with the Three Lions’ under-20 side, which would have potentially meant they'd have the game postponed due to international call-ups.

Of course, Stockport couldn’t enforce that, but in a fresh development shared by journalist Sam Byrne, it has been confirmed that the 19-year-old has withdrawn from international duty.

Which League One sides have been making the most noise so far in 2026-27?

If Whatmuff had remained with England, Stockport would’ve been forced to look into the emergency loan market to bring in a player for seven days to play against the Royals.

Obviously, it’s an honour for Whatmuff to be picked to play for England at youth level, and he will no doubt enjoy linking up with the national team.

But, the reality is that playing against Reading in a competitive League One game is going to be better for his development than featuring in games against the U20 sides of Czechia and Portugal.

So, Moore and England deserve credit for putting the needs of Stockport and the player ahead of themselves.

This is good news for Stockport, and, as outlined above, it’s something that should benefit the player as well.

Now, though, Whatmuff will be focused on the Reading game, and all connected to County will see this as a great opportunity to take advantage in the promotion race.

That’s because a host of Stockport’s rivals at the top aren’t playing, and McNulty’s side know that they can go joint-top with Huddersfield if they pick up three points against Reading.

Car Insurance Soars for Drivers With Poor Credit; Here’s What to Do

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

We believe everyone should be able to make financial decisions with confidence. While we don't cover every company or financial product on the market, we work hard to share a wide range of offers and objective editorial perspectives.

So how do we make money? Our partners compensate us for advertisements that appear on our site. This compensation helps us provide tools and services - like free credit score access and monitoring. With the exception of mortgage, home equity and other home-lending products or services, partner compensation is one of several factors that may affect which products we highlight and where they appear on our site. Other factors include your credit profile, product availability and proprietary website methodologies.

However, these factors do not influence our editors' opinions or ratings, which are based on independent research and analysis. Our partners cannot pay us to guarantee favorable reviews. Here is a list of our partners.

While building your credit, shop around for auto insurance quotes. Some insurers are more lenient than others.

Lead Writer & Licensed Insurance Specialist

Your driving record may be spotless, but if you have poor credit, car insurance companies view you as a risk — and they’re significantly raising your rates.

Over the past three years, drivers with poor credit have seen auto insurance premiums surge 37.7%, according to NerdWallet’s 2026 Auto Insurance Rates Report. These price hikes were even steeper than those faced by drivers with good credit who caused a crash or got a DUI.

In January 2023, someone with poor credit and a clean driving record would pay a median of $2,864 per year for full-coverage car insurance, NerdWallet’s analysis found. By January 2026, premiums for this driver had risen to $3,944 — an increase of $1,081 per year. By comparison, the same driver with good credit saw an increase of only $576.

“Credit scores are one of the most harmful things that results in you paying higher auto insurance premiums,” says Michael DeLong, a research and advocacy associate with the Washington, D.C.-based nonprofit Consumer Federation of America. “The penalties are extremely severe.”

While higher premiums after a wreck or DUI may not be a surprise, DeLong says many drivers aren’t aware their credit scores will affect their car insurance rates. To further complicate things, building credit can take months.

But there are ways to get relief more quickly. DeLong suggests comparing car insurance quotes from multiple companies, because some insurers penalize poor credit less than others. Raising your deductible, evaluating your coverage and avoiding small claims can also help.

NerdWallet’s analysis found that insurers raised rates more sharply from 2023 to 2026 for drivers with poor credit than for those with good credit — even if the poor-credit driver had a flawless record while the good-credit driver had a DUI, speeding ticket or at-fault crash.

When our sample driver with poor credit also had a recent DUI or speeding ticket, rate increases were even greater. Prices for a full-coverage policy went up by more than 40% for drivers with poor credit and a recent DUI, the biggest increase of any profile we analyzed.

Drivers often think it’s unfair for credit to affect car insurance prices, DeLong says, since it isn’t directly linked to driving behavior. Some states agree; California, Hawaii and Massachusetts don’t allow credit as a factor in setting auto insurance rates.

However, insurers say credit scores can indicate the likelihood that a driver will file a claim.[1]National Association of Insurance Commissioners. Credit-Based Insurance Scores. Accessed Nov 2, 2026. View all sources They use what’s called a credit-based insurance score — similar to the typical credit scores used by lenders but weighted differently.

“It helps in predicting expected claims activity and loss costs more accurately,” said Norman Niami, a member of the American Academy of Actuaries' Automobile Insurance Committee, in an email.

The practice has been controversial, with critics arguing it disproportionately hurts low-income and minority drivers. But a Federal Trade Commission report to Congress in July 2007 concluded that credit-based insurance scores were strong predictors of risk even when controlled for income, race and ethnicity.[2]A Report to Congress by the Federal Trade Commission. Credit-Based Insurance Scores: Impacts on Consumers of Automobile Insurance. Accessed Nov 2, 2026. View all sources

The explanation may be that some people simply tend to take risks, according to a statistical analysis published in the North American Actuarial Journal in 2016.[3]North American Actuarial Journal. Empirical Evidence on the Use of Credit Scoring for Predicting Insurance Losses with Psycho-social and Biochemical Explanations. Accessed Nov 2, 2026. View all sources Researchers concluded that risk-taking behavior can affect credit scores as well as insurance claims. Their analysis found that average losses per policy were 64.6% higher for drivers with the lowest credit scores than for those with the best credit.

Working to build your credit over time may help lower your car insurance premiums, but it’s not a sure thing. A credit-based insurance score is just one of many factors (along with things like location, age and driving history) that insurers use to rate a person’s risk.

Building credit “can produce a noticeable premium decrease in some cases, while producing little or no change in others,” Niami said, depending on whether the new score causes an insurer to move you into a better rating tier. Each insurer sets its own score thresholds, so a score viewed as “poor” by one insurer might be considered “fair” by another.

At the same time, consumer advocates argue it’s difficult for many people to take the steps needed to strengthen their credit scores.

“Most people probably have some idea that you can improve your credit score by trying to pay your bills regularly and reliably, and using different kinds of financial resources and credit cards,” DeLong says.

“But again, that’s easier said than done," he says. "If you’re struggling at a low-wage job and trying to juggle bills, or if you don’t easily have access to a credit card, that advice may not be especially helpful."

Drivers with poor credit or no credit may be able to take other steps to lower their premiums.

Comparing car insurance quotes from different companies is one of the best ways to ensure you have the cheapest rate possible. Insurers set their own pricing formulas, and some are more forgiving of poor credit than others. A NerdWallet analysis of October 2026 rates found that in about half (49.3%) of U.S. ZIP codes in states that allow credit scoring, the cheapest insurer for a driver with poor credit was a different company than the cheapest for a driver with good credit.

Although you can’t improve your risk profile overnight, “there’s a bunch of small things you can do that over a two- to three-year span make you look a lot better” to insurers, says Bradley Flowers, founder of Portal Insurance agency in Mobile, Alabama.

Consider a higher deductible. Raising your car insurance deductible — the amount of a future claim that you agree to cover yourself — lowers your premium and signals to insurers that you’re less likely to file a claim. But be sure you can afford to pay the higher deductible if you have to make a claim.

Adjust how you’re billed by insurers. By signing up for automatic payments and ensuring you don’t miss insurance payments each month, you’ll get a small discount while improving your risk profile for insurers.

Avoid minor claims if you can. Flowers recommends thinking twice about filing minor claims, such as windshield cracks. “When you’re in one of these situations, let’s save our insurance for the really bad stuff,” he suggests.

Lastly, be sure your coverage fits your vehicle. If you could afford to replace your car on your own, consider dropping collision and comprehensive coverage to bring your premiums down. Your trimmed-down policy would still provide you with liability coverage after a crash, while leaving you to cover damage to your vehicle.

NerdWallet's 2026 Auto Insurance Rates Report is based on an analysis of more than 1.4 billion rate estimates provided by Quadrant Information Services for all 50 states and Washington, D.C., from January 2023 to January 2026.

Quadrant derives its rate data from modeled estimates based on carrier rating algorithms; figures do not reflect every available carrier or every consumer's individual rate. Liberty Mutual does not participate in Quadrant's rate data and is not represented in this analysis.

Quadrant’s figures are modeled rate estimates produced by running a fixed driver profile through carrier rating algorithms. They represent both new business and renewal rate estimates, and they are not a record of what actual drivers paid.

NerdWallet anchored its analysis to a 3-year old Toyota Camry LE, ensuring each analysis period reflected rates for the same-aged car.

The primary rate analysis is based on a 35-year-old single driver with good credit, no recent at-fault accidents or driving violations, and a full-coverage policy with $1,000 deductibles for collision and comprehensive coverage. Rates are pooled across male and female drivers.

In states where drivers must carry other types of insurance not included in our full coverage profile specifications, we added the minimum amount of coverage required by those states. Some policies include additional coverage at the insurer’s discretion.

In addition to our base profile driver with good credit and a spotless driving history, we analyzed rates for the following driver profiles:

Drivers with an at-fault crash. We added a single at-fault crash causing $10,000 in property damage.

Drivers with a speeding ticket. We added a single speeding ticket for driving 16 mph over the speed limit.

Drivers with a DUI. We added a single drunken-driving violation.

Drivers with poor credit. This is the same as our regular base profile, except the driver has poor credit instead of good credit. We also analyzed rates for drivers with poor credit and each of the three incidents above.

NerdWallet applied a strict comparability filter before calculating year-over-year figures. Only coverage variants and carrier/state combinations present in all four years of data were included in median calculations. This ensures reported changes reflect genuine pricing shifts among stable carriers, rather than differences caused by insurers entering markets, exiting, or going insolvent during the analysis period.

Your ‘Healthy’ Soda Alternative May Not Be So Healthy for Your Liver

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

WE'RE LONG PAST having to make a decision between just Coke or Pepsi. You've got a lot of options if you're bored of water—from fizzy nonalcoholic drinks to flavored seltzers. But while a lot of these drinks market themselves as healthy alternatives to sodas, research suggests they could be silently doing a number on your liver health.

If you've done the work to switch to diet sodas or have learned to love prebiotic sodas like Olipop or Poppi, the news can be pretty hard to hear. So it's important to get this out of the way upfront: If you have a "healthy" soda every once in a while, the experts we spoke to say the risk of having liver problems is unlikely. The real danger to your liver is if you’re tossing these back on a daily or near-daily basis. Here's everything you need to know.

THE STUDY PRESENTED at the United European Gastroenterology (UEG) Week 2025 used data from 103,251 people who enrolled in the UK Biobank and didn’t have liver disease at the start of the study. Participants were asked to fill out questionnaires about what they ate and drank throughout the day. The researchers then paid close attention to how much sugar-sweetened and low- or non-sugar-sweetened beverages they drank.

The major takeaway from the study is that it pretty much linked any drink that wasn’t water to metabolic dysfunction-associated steatotic liver disease (MASLD). You might know it by its former name, nonalcoholic fatty liver disease (NAFLD). But whatever you call it, it's no joke.

MASLD happens when too much fat builds up in your liver from inputs other than alcohol. It can lead to serious liver scarring or end-stage liver disease. It can also put you at high risk for heart disease and diabetes. According to the American College of Gastroenterology, heart disease is the number one killer of people living with MASLD.

DAILY DRINKING OF nearly 9 ounces of any type of drink that was not water was associated with a significantly higher risk of developing MASLD. That risk jumped 50 percent for sugar-sweetened beverages and was surprisingly higher—60 percent—for those who drank low- or non-sugar-sweetened beverages. (Though some of these prebiotic sodas have more sugar and less fiber than marketing claims might imply).

After a 10-year follow-up, 949 people developed MASLD and 103 died from liver-related causes. Both types of beverages were significantly connected to increases in liver fat.

Replacing nearly 12 ounces of sugary beverages with water lowered MASLD risk by 14.4 percent and by 13.5 percent with low- or non-sugar-sweetened drinks. But if you switched completely from a sugary beverage like soda to a low-sugar beverage like fruit juice, the authors found that this did not help lower a person's risk of developing MASLD.

Exactly which beverages raised risk is hard to say because the authors did not directly state what low- or no-sugar drinks people were drinking. That said, there are lots of sodas that are marketed as being healthier alternatives that are not as good as they claim, according to Danbee Kim, MD, nutrition expert and assistant professor at Rutgers New Jersey Medical School.

“Even beverages marketed as ‘natural’ or ‘health-oriented’ may still fall into this category if they rely on sweeteners instead of sugar,” Kim says. “The key commonality is that they provide sweetness without significant calories. However, they may still affect liver health.”

A FEW THINGS may be going on here. “Low/non-sugar sweetened beverages, although low in calories, may still affect liver health through several pathways,” says Lihe Liu, a graduate student researcher at The First Affiliated Hospital of Soochow University in China and lead study author. “They can alter the gut microbiome, interfere with feelings of fullness, increase preference for sweet foods, and in some cases stimulate insulin secretion.”

When you have something that’s sweet, even if it doesn’t contain much or any sugar, it sets off a chain reaction in your body the same way as if you were to actually have sugar, says Albert Matheny, RD, CSCS, registered dietitian and co-founder of SoHo Strength Lab. “You’re basically tricking your body,” he says. “Your body still reacts as if it has sugar.”

As a result, Matheny says your body produces more insulin (a hormone that helps regulate blood sugar), and encourages the liver to take in more sugar from your bloodstream. This can lead to metabolic disruption.

“Some artificial sweeteners may alter how the body handles glucose and insulin, contributing to insulin resistance, which is a key driver of metabolic dysfunction and fat accumulation in the liver,” says Kim. This process may even mess with your appetite, making you think that you're hungry when you're not.

Another reason has nothing to do with your gut, but with your mind. Kim says we can't discount the possibility that people who drink these low- and non-sugar-sweetened beverages may subconsciously compensate for the “saved” calories by eating more sugary or fatty foods later.

Keep in mind that all this is speculation. The explanation for why and how artificially sweetened beverages are linked to MASLD is still under investigation since Liu says her team didn't look too deep into this.

NOT NECESSARILY. “THEY’RE definitely an improvement over traditional diet sodas,” Matheny says. “That’s not to say that they’re a totally healthy option.”

When given a choice, water reigns supreme. And even then you still don't want to go too overboard.

If you still want to crack open a cranberry fizz prebiotic soda or even an unsweetened iced tea, Matheny says that’s probably fine, too. “In moderation, kind of everything is OK.”

Korin Miller is a seasoned freelance writer specializing in general wellness, sexual health and relationships, and lifestyle trends, with work appearing in Men’s Health, Women’s Health, Everyday Health, Food & Wine, and more. She has a master’s degree from American University and lives by the beach.

I Asked Designers for Their Secrets to an Expensive-Looking Guest Bedroom. They All Said the Same Thing

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Skip the fussy pillow pile and focus on the details guests will actually notice.

Pictured above: a bold aubergine double guest bedroom designed by Nicole Fisher.

The holiday hosting season is upon us, and while you’re planning your tablescape decor and seasonal trimmings, you might want to take a hard look at your guest bedroom.

“Most guest rooms get treated like overflow storage, and guests feel that the second they walk in,” says Marie Cloud, principal designer at Indigo Pruitt.

Even if you host only occasionally, your guest room should give visitors a comfortable place to unwind. No need for a guest book or fuzzy slippers: Designers say these four thoughtful touches can make the room feel luxe without costing a fortune.

Regardless of the design aesthetic, the ambiance of a room always hinges on lighting. “I always pay attention to the lighting,” says Louis Lin, founder of his eponymous New York City–based interior design studio. “A beautiful bedside lamp with a warm, flattering glow can transform the atmosphere of a room. Layering different light sources, rather than relying on overhead lighting, can do that. It's a relatively simple gesture that makes a room feel much more intimate and refined.” This also gives guests the freedom to tailor the air of their space to their liking. It’s one of the few details they can truly control in their temporary lodging.

While a pile of pillows might look inviting, designer Marie Cloud says the bedtime legwork promptly cancels out the aesthetics.

“Skip the stack of eight decorative pillows your guest has to relocate before bed,” says Cloud. Her perfect formula for a top-notch guest setup? Two sleeping pillows, plus one lumbar pillow, all set atop a good set of sheets.

“Sheets with real hand-feel, like a crisp percale or washed linen,” Cloud suggests. “Finish with a throw folded at the foot in a color pulled from the room. It looks considered, and it’s actually comfortable at 2 a.m.”

Most guest rooms get the all-white wall treatment in the name of neutrality, but all it really says is that your space lacks intention. Just because this room is (mostly) reserved for guests doesn't mean it has to lack character. In fact, Cloud says you should not hold back.

“Drench the room,” says the designer. “Walls, trim, and ceiling in the same color, ideally something deep and warm. Continuous color reads custom, it makes a small room feel wrapped rather than cramped, and it tells your guest the space was designed for them. A guest room is also the lowest-risk room in the house to be bold, so take the swing.”

The best guest setups make visitors feel anticipated, even when they show up unexpectedly. To achieve the boutique-hotel feel, designers recommend investing in small luxuries that leave a big impact. A few items Cloud and Lin always recommend having on hand are a small luggage rack, real hangers, fresh towels, and a select set of toiletries reserved especially for guests.

“The real luxury is making guests feel that the room has been prepared just for them,” says Lin.

Nanobody Shows Promise in Myasthenia Gravis

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

— PREVAIL trial found rapid improvement with a once-weekly subcutaneous injection

Results from the phase III PREVAIL trial presented at the American Association of Neuromuscular & Electrodiagnostic Medicine annual meeting showed that treatment with the investigational once-weekly nanobody gefurulimab significantly improved symptoms in adults with anti-acetylcholine receptor antibody-positive generalized myasthenia gravis, with a safety profile generally comparable to placebo.

In this exclusive MedPage Today video, James Winkley, MD, of Baptist Health Medical Group in Lexington, Kentucky, discusses the findings, including the potential convenience of self-administered treatment with the subcutaneous injectable and compatibility with intravenous immunoglobulin (IVIG) therapy.

Following is a transcript of his remarks:

The PREVAIL study was a double-blind, placebo-controlled trial with gefurulimab as the treating agent. Gefurulimab is a unique agent in that it's the first nanobody that'll be used in myasthenia gravis, which makes it a smaller molecule, which allows for greater tissue penetration and solubility -- so it can be given as a [subcutaneous] injection rather than the larger monoclonal antibodies, which have to be given in larger volumes underneath the skin or as IV infusion. So this would represent a greater ease of access for patients to have a [subcutaneous], once-a-week injection that doesn't require them to go to the infusion center.

As we had seen in the trial, similar to eculizumab [Bkemv] and ravulizumab [Ultomiris], which are C5 inhibitors, it met its primary outcome of MG-ADL [Myasthenia Gravis Activities of Daily Living] reduction, with a minus 4.2 reduction versus 2.1 on placebo, which was statistically significant. We did see a quick onset of action within 1 week, with a statistically significant reduction in the MG-ADL for those patients, as well as 4 weeks for the [Quantitative Myasthenia Gravis].

There were similar side effects between the treatment group and the placebo group, with injection-site reactions being slightly higher in the treatment group, but all of those reactions tended to be mild to moderate. The most common adverse events were headaches, upper respiratory infections, and muscle aches and pains. One thing that was significant at any time we're using complement inhibitors, there were no meningococcal infections. Everybody made sure they were fully vaccinated, and if they'd had to be started prior to 2 weeks, they received antibiotics. But we had a very typical safety profile, as I said, between the placebo arm and the treatment arm -- the side effects were similar.

This is going to make for ease of access for patients. You still have other molecules out on the market. You've got a once-daily complement inhibitor and you've got now a once-every-2-weeks IV infusion versus every 8 weeks. So kind of depending on what the patient prefers, this gives them greater access. We've got an easily portable complement inhibitor that patients no longer are tethered to an infusion center that they have to worry about. When's our next infusion? Do I have access? And I think that's going to be a huge advantage for the patients and their quality of life.

I would say that one thing that's unique about [gefurulimab] is that it is compatible with IVIG. And so the other larger monoclonal antibodies like the FcRn [neonatal Fc receptor] and the larger eculizumab and ravulizumab, it gets recycled by a different mechanism because it goes through the albumin on the FcRn receptor. It was combined with IVIG during the trial, patients could be on stable dosages of it, so it has that advantage of being able to be compared and not have to give supplemental dosing.

So I think that it will have combination with other available medications. I think that's a big advantage for it.

U.S. gas prices fall below $4 for the first time since July

📅 2026-11-10  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Americans may finally find some relief at the pump.

The national average price of regular gasoline fell below $4 a gallon for the first time since July, reaching $3.9788 on Tuesday, according to AAA. Prices are down about 17 cents from a week ago and nearly 39 cents from a month ago.

The decline comes after energy inflation slowed significantly in October. Energy prices increased 0.7% during the month, a far cry from the 5.2% surge seen in September, according to the Bureau of Labor Statistics. The BLS' gasoline index increased 1% over the month, after advancing 9.1% in September.

Bespoke Investment Group pointed out that gasoline prices also tend to decline at this time of the year, making part of the recent relief seasonal.

"While lower oil prices have been a tailwind for the relief at the pump, it's also perfectly normal for gas prices to be falling at this time of year, so if they weren't, there would really be something wrong," the firm said.

The drop at the pump followed a sharp retreat in crude oil. International oil benchmark Brent crude on Tuesday briefly fell below $90 a barrel for the first time since August as comments from U.S. President Donald Trump fueled hopes for an end to Washington's nearly nine-month conflict with Iran.

Recovering oil flows from the Persian Gulf have also eased supply concerns. Regional crude exports have returned to an estimated 80% to 85% of prewar levels, including pipeline shipments, according to ING.

"Energy markets remain under pressure as hopes for a de-escalation in the Middle East reduce geopolitical risk premiums. Oil extended its losses as Gulf supply flows continued to recover, while European gas also moved lower," analysts at ING wrote.

However, the Federal Reserve Governor Christopher Waller warned Tuesday that energy shocks may have more persistent effects on inflation than they did before the pandemic.

"While I am pleased to see energy prices down from their high rates over the past week or so, they are still quite elevated relative to the start of the year, and the ultimate effect on inflation will depend on what levels are sustained," he said.

Don't be fooled by Horses of Hoofprint Bay's hand-drawn art, this stable management game has all the drama of a Jilly Cooper novel

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Poema is my pride and joy. She was my first horse and she's a competition winner, but she's reached her potential. She'll never be a show jumper, and that's where the money and prestige are to be found. If I'm ever to grow my horse farm into a nationally renowned equestrian centre, I need the cash to expand and upgrade my facilities. Poema is a hoofstep on the path to that future, but she's not going to be able to take me all the way herself.

For all its hand-drawn aesthetics, Horses of Hoofprint Bay has hard realities like the limits of Poema's future bubbling under its surface. While the game's scripted narrative may be lacking, it's shockingly easy to fill in the gaps with drama worthy of a Jilly Cooper novel.

There may be better author comparisons, but I learned everything I know about showjumping from Cooper's Riders – much as I learned everything about independent television franchise bids in the '80s from its sequel Rivals – a book which is as much about the nitty gritty of horsetraining and competing as it is about toffs jumping into bed with one another. In my mind the two are tightly connected - showjumping and Jilly Cooper, not horses and toff bonking. In Horses of Hoofprint Bay, the only real story you get is the fact you've scrimped and saved to buy your first horse and your uncle has lent you the use of his field while you start your business. I was hoping there would at least be a similar setup to Regency Solitaire, where your motivation for touring the country's tearooms is motivated by paying off your brother's gambling debts. Yet, despite this disappointing start, I soon found its business simulation mechanics provide great storytelling material.

Horses of Hoofprint Bay has many of the trappings of a cosy game. At the start of each day, you'll prepare the stables, dragging a rake around the yard to sling away the manure, clicking buckets of water to refill the trough, and making sure there's enough hay for all the horses. But this isn't just for the larks, if you skip these tasks there are serious knock on effects for yours horses. Forget to brush down your horses' coats or pick out the stones in their shoes, and they're liable to develop saddle sores and other injuries when you ride them. Leave an abrasion untreated and it can develop into an infection. Even something like taking a dirty horse to a contest can cost you. I forgot to brush Poema before entering her into her first contest and despite taking first place, her embarrassingly unkempt coat lost my stable prestige. It was a cruel end to what should have been a great celebration.

So long as your horses are well rested, you can train them each day. You may want to take them through some confidence building lunges, where they need to walk, trot, and canter on demand, or a more physically demanding Cavaletti or showjumping course. These latter too are much more tiring, but they build a horse's strength and endurance. The higher its endurance the more energy it can expend on training sessions each day, and the higher its strength the more jumps it can do in succession before slowing to a walk for a breather - essential when you're competing in a timed competition. Overdo the exercise, however, and you'll exhaust a horse, severely reducing its energy for the next day.

Now, unlike Poema, my freshly bought stallion Kolizey has the potential to compete in showjumping competitions. He'll never go all the way to the big national contests, but he can take a ribbon in the district and regional contests. Or he will when he's up to standard; he's arrived from the breeder in a sorry state: brambles in his mane, mud on his flanks, stones in his shoes, and his strength and endurance are far too low to make it through an entire course of obstacles.

The biggest problem, however, is Kolizey's confidence: it's shot. I don't know what was happening in the stalls at the breeder's, but he arrived at my stables backing up at the first sign of a jump. Before I can hope to earn him his first ribbon, I have to build up our relationship. If we exercise together enough in the yard, his confidence will grow and he will follow my direction without question.

It may be getting ahead of myself, but I also have plans for Kolizey and Poema to produce a foal. Done right, the two should raise a horse that's able to exceed its parents' potential, becoming stronger and more endurant than both of them. A stronger horse means a ticket to the bigger contests and the bigger prize pots. Not that I can start breeding the two just yet. I want both horses to be at peak physical fitness. Poema's already there, while Kolizey can't even manage a jump without shying away.

While getting Koliszey contest ready, I still need to be bringing in cash. That means using Poema as a training horse for students' riding lessons. The better I am at the minigame where I use the Q, E, W, A, S, and D keys to tell them to sit forward or back, hold the reins higher or lower, or to level out their legs, the more they tip me. I have to be careful, because when Poema is working with students, she's not being stretched, and her strength and endurance can wither which means she struggles to maintain her winning streak in the contests.

Another reason to hold off on getting Poema pregnant is that during the second half of her pregnancy, she can't be ridden, which means no contests and no lessons for students. Currently, those are my two main sources of income besides what I'm paid in rent to board a couple of nearby farmers' horses. I don't want her out of the rota when Kolizey isn't yet ready to step into her place.

And, well, while I'm on the subject of plans, reader, I must tell you, the future lies in semen. If I can build the facilities, earn the cash, and raise Kolizey's profile, I can make a good buck on his virile stallion cream. With the money I bring in from a prizewinning semen I can expand into the far field, creating space to take on more horses, upgrade my home from the caravan I'm living in currently, maybe even build a proper training school. I'm not going to afford all that by giving riding lessons to the local children or boarders' rent. No, the big money comes from competition wins and valuable horse sauce.

While Poema continues entering contests and carrying students, I start pushing Kolizey harder. Multiple training sessions every day. I begin with Cavaletti, which is a kind of obedience competition. Your horse has to be travelling at the correct speed when it passes marker flags – walking, trotting, or cantering – and make some small jumps over cross bars. It should be straightforward, but because Kolizey's confidence is so low, he keeps refusing my orders and trying to change speed. He warns me before he's about to speed up or slow down, and if I counter it in time he will obey me and even receive a little confidence boost, but often he does it just before a jump, messing up my timing and he hits the bars. Each time he fails a jump it knocks his confidence, risking me taking me even further from where I need him to be.

I try the easier lunges sessions. And they raise his confidence a little but they're doing little for his physical health. It's all taking too long and Poema's getting older every day. In a fit of frustration, and in mark that I may not be able to cut it as an animal trainer, I skip from the Cavaletti training straight into a showjumping qualifier. The time limits on these are much stricter, so you have to clear the course at a gallop. This is also where a horse's strength really comes into play. The faster you're going, the more strength it requires to jump an obstacle. Horses only recover strength when moving at a walk, so you don't want to tire a horse out by going at a gallop through the whole course, else they won't have the strength to make all the jumps and they'll be forced to withdraw from the event. But nor do you want to go too slowly and fall foul of the time limit.

I realise my mistake as soon as the course begins: I try to stir Kolizey into a walk he refuses. Thankfully the timer only starts when he crosses the line, but it's an inauspicious start. I tap forward again and he begins to walk. I tap again and Kolizey moves up to a trot, then to a canter, before picking up to a gallop. I think he's going too fast as we approach the first jump, but Kolizey clears it. Then another jump and another. With a nervous eye on the depleting strength bar, I tap him down to a canter for the next jump. We're halfway through the course, I briefly slow to a walk to recover some strength then take the next three jumps at a canter. We're so close to the end now but a big yellow exclamation mark appears next to the strength bar. Kolizey is on the edge of exhaustion but he refuses to slow down and increases to a gallop for the last two jumps.

He completes the course with a perfect clear. Not a single obstacle clipped and we end with ten seconds left on the timer. Not only has Kolizey earned his showjumping license, his success swells his confidence. He's a natural athlete.

The next day, with my horses rested, I take Kolizey and Poema to town to compete in the showjumping and Caprilli events respectively. They both bring home first place, raising the prestige of my stables, earning me a tidy little packet, and convincing me it's time my prized horses started a family. I'm perhaps too eager, when I try to send them to the breeding station, I'm told they're both 'Done for the day'. I suppose I should give my contest winners a bit of a break. They've earned it. I groom them and send them both to bed with an apple as a treat.

Except, maybe they weren't too tired: the next morning, I'm met with a message telling me Poema is pregnant. My stable's become the Olympic Village, with my rosette-winning horses working out the highs and stresses of competition in the only way they know how. Good on them. I needn't have even made the breeding station.

Then, with horror, I remember the horses I'm boarding for the neighbours. They're both stallions themselves. Neither of them are at competition level, their strength and endurance is lower even than Poema's when she arrived at the stable. If either of them are the father, Poema is about to be out of action for a whole season and at the other end of it I'll have a foal that can't even compete in the beginner events. fetch the equestrian version of Hercule Poirot, I need to find out who impregnated my horse!

It may not be a plot worthy of Cooper, but it will do for me.

The 7 Airlines Flying Airbus A321XLRs On Both Short & Long-Haul Routes

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

In evolutionary biology, conversation is often focused on 'the' thing features evolved for. For example, bird wings evolved for flying. In reality, birds will use them for whatever they can. Penguins use them for swimming, while chickens use them for changing direction while running and shading their chicks.

Aircraft are somewhat similar. They may be optimized for a specific mission, but that doesn't mean they will be used for those missions by the airline all the time. In Vietnam, VietJet sometimes flies its widebody A330s on ultra-short domestic routes from Ho Chi Minh City to Nha Trang half empty. VietJet apparently does this because the time slot is available in the A330's international schedule.

The Airbus A321XLR is optimized for longer, but thinner routes. On shorter routes, its increased weight from its extra tanks and strengthened landing gear imposes a penalty. Even so, some airlines will operate them on short routes, partly because they are available. Let's examine some of the airlines using the XLR on both short and longer-haul routes.

Aer Lingus is the flag carrier of Ireland and has a fleet of six Airbus A321XLRs. These are configured with 16 business class seats and 168 economy class seats for a total of 184 seats. This is identical to its complementary fleet of eight A321LRs. In July 2026, Aer Lingus flew more than 300 flights longer than 3,100 miles (5,000 kilometers) and more than 200 routes shorter than 1,250 miles (2,000 kilometers) with its XLRs.

Those shorter routes are European hops when the aircraft are not flying their longer transatlantic trips. An example of a long Aer Lingus XLR flight is Dublin to Nashville, which comes with a scheduled flight time of nine hours and 20 minutes. Meanwhile, during the first week of November, the XLR (registered EI-XLU) has flown hops such as Dublin to London (48 minutes) and Dublin to Barcelona (one hour and 50 minutes).

Flightradar24 shows EI-XLU's longer recent flights include Nashville (Tennessee), Cleveland (Ohio), Toronto (Canada), Indianapolis (Indiana), and New York. Being located at the far western edge of Europe, Aer Lingus has the advantage of being able to reach more destinations in the US with the XLR. The XLR is an excellent aircraft for thinner transatlantic missions, but it is also ok to use on short hops, accepting a modest penalty.

Iberia was the launch customer for the Airbus A321XLR in late 2024, and today it operates a fleet of seven examples with an eighth on order. These are configured with 14 business class seats and 168 economy seats, totaling 182 seats. Spain (excluding Russia) leads Europe with the most domestic flights, driven by the need to connect the mainland with the Canary Islands and Balearic Islands.

From its base in Madrid, Iberia flies its A321XLRs to transatlantic destinations in the United States such as Boston, Newark, Washington DC, and Canada's Toronto. Having a more Spanish and Latin focus than carriers like the UK or Ireland, it also flies them to the Dominican Republic (Santo Domingo) and Puerto Rico (San Juan) in the Caribbean (both Spanish-speaking).

In South America, its XLRs fly as far as Recife and Fortaleza in Brazil. Recent flight tracking data for the XLR registered EC-OOJ reveals it is mostly flying longer transatlantic routes. However, it has also flown European hops to Zurich and Paris (both one hour and 30 minutes) as well as Venice (two hours).

Qantas will have one of the highest passenger capacities onboard its Airbus A321XLRs.

Air Canada is one of the XLR's newest operators, with the national Canadian airline flying the aircraft since June. Now the airline has a growing fleet of five to six in service. Air Canada's official website says it has six in inventory, while Planespotters.net still records five delivered, of which one is currently grounded. That aircraft is brand new and working up.

Regardless, Air Canada is in the process of building up one of the world's largest XLR fleets, with around 24 of its 30-strong order still to be delivered. Air Canada is using the aircraft on a range of longer routes, including transatlantic flights to Europe. C-GXLR was the first XLR delivered to Air Canada. In recent weeks, its longest flight was Toronto to Copenhagen (up to eight hours),

Other longer routes include Montreal to Toulouse (about seven hours), as well as Montreal to Lisbon, Montreal to Porto, and other European destinations. It is also used extensively on medium (three- to five-hour) flights to various destinations in the United States and western Canada such as Los Angeles and Calgary. Ultra short hops include Ottawa to Toronto (39 minutes) and Montreal to Toronto (53 minutes).

Qantas is another of the leading Airbus A321XLR customers, with a total of 48 on order or delivered. The Flying Kangaroo is currently taking delivery of its XLRs, with seven now in inventory. These are ideally suited to some Pacific routes where the range is vast, but the routes are thin. The fact that Qantas ordered 20 more XLRs in August 2025 suggests it has many Asia-Pacific routes in mind.

However, few have been announced. The XLR registered VH-OGA was the first delivered to Qantas in April 2025. Confusingly, its flight history over the last month shows it has only been used on domestic routes. The shortest route is the one-hour flight between Sydney and Melbourne, while the longest flight is the domestic transcontinental flight from Sydney to Perth, which is less than five hours.

Qantas is introducing the type progressively and assigning it to domestic trunk routes before expanding internationally. Upcoming international routes include Perth to India (expected), Adelaide to Singapore, and Brisbane to Manila. While Qantas has not announced all of its upcoming Pacific routes, interesting destinations include Fiji, Samoa, the Cook Islands, and Tahiti.

American Airlines now has 11 of its 40 XLRs ordered in inventory. As with European carriers, American is assigning these to transatlantic routes. They also have the range to reach many leisure and thinner destinations in the Caribbean, Central America, and South America.

The first XLR American took delivery of is N300NY. Its most recent flight history is dominated by transcontinental routes flying from Boston or New York to Los Angeles or San Francisco, taking around five hours. It is also flying transatlantic routes from New York to Barcelona (up to eight hours) and New York to Edinburgh (up to six hours).

Largest publicly reported A321XLR customers

While short hops appear less common in American's fleet, there are some. One XLR (N301NY) has flown hops like Boston to Philadelphia (one hour). As with other airlines, the American XLR fleet is working up and is yet to take on many of its intended routes. South America is identified as a future market with potential American routes including Miami to Porto Alegre, Brasilia, Belo Horizonte, and others.

The Airbus321XLR will change the mid-sized market and allow new destinations for airlines.

United Airlines is another major airline busy inducting its new XLR fleet and gearing up for transatlantic and other routes. It has recently suggested that the XLR lacks the range to serve some deeper European routes, such as those to Greece. This is somewhat confusing, as it is well within the advertised range and other airlines fly comparable distances.

United has not provided a detailed breakdown of its calculations, including payload, required margins, routing, etc., to understand the assertion. It has just received the first three of its 50-strong order, leaving 47 remaining on the order book. These are now starting to replace its aging fleet of 757-200s.

With the first delivered in May, United's XLR remains a fleet in its infancy. Flight records show they have yet to fly longer transatlantic routes to Western Europe, although they are coming. Mostly, the flights have been transcontinental, around four to five hours. One short hop in the record is San Diego to Denver, a flight time of less than two hours.

IndiGo is the leading low-cost carrier of India and is one of the world's fastest-rising carriers. It boasts a fleet of 425 aircraft in service and has 881 more on order. While delivered, that will make it one of the largest airlines in the world in the 2030s. The airline's fleet is overwhelmingly A320 family narrowbodies, although it has 60 A350 widebodies on order.

IndiGo has 175 A321neos in service plus four new XLRs. It has the largest order for the XLR, totalling 69 examples. This is made up of the 40 original firm orders publicly confirmed by IndiGo and 29 additional aircraft reflected in industry fleet databases. Once again, the fleet is in its infancy and is working up.

Found an error? Send it info@simpleflying.com so it can be corrected.

With that being said, IndiGo seems much faster at deploying them on its longer-haul routes than other airlines. Flight history for VT-NLA shows it has been flying between Delhi and Athens (over eight hours), Mumbai and Istanbul (over seven hours), Mumbai and Amsterdam (up to nine hours and 30 minutes). One example of a short hop is the two-hour route between Delhi and Mumbai.

‘Chasing Summer’ Director Josephine Decker Says That ‘Filmmaking Is Lonelier Than Ever,’ So It’s Time to Build a Community

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

“I have this crescendoing dream of creating a residency for movies without scripts. That’s how I made my short ‘A Stable Marriage.’ We basically had one paragraph, and then we spent a whole day improvising together. It was so much fun and so refreshing,” she tells Variety.

Decker has directed award-winning dramas “Madeline’s Madeline” and “Shirley” with Elisabeth Moss. Related Stories Japan IP Market to Return to Cannes in 2027 as Tokyo Partnership Continues Guillermo del Toro Tears Up as He Says ‘We’re Handing Down a World in Ruins’: ‘It Pains Me Deeply to See Three Generations Fail the Next’

She’s adamant – when the industry is struggling, a sense of community is everything.

“I don’t write alone. I either write with a community or build stuff with actors. Of all my films, ‘Madeline’s Madeline’ is probably closest to my heart because I never felt like the idea was totally mine. It emerged from many people and many minds, not just one,” she says.

At this year’s Tauron American Film Festival, Decker will receive the Indie Star Award. Previous recipients include Jesse Eisenberg, John Waters and Christine Vachon.

Recently, Decker made “Chasing Summer” with star comedian Iliza Shlesinger.

“I think Iliza was interested in working with me because she wanted the film to feel artistic. I was thinking: ‘What’s an art comedy?’ ‘Punch-Drunk Love’ was the only one I could think of, so I watched it a bunch of times. It was fun to push myself in that way. I don’t always do that,” she recalls.

Urszula Śniegowska, director of the festival, says, “Her stories and protagonists, mostly female artists, illustrate Josephine’s own struggles as a woman in a changing art world. This retrospective has been a long time coming, and the new film provided the perfect opportunity to celebrate her complete body of work.”

Decker grew up watching ambitious films. Maybe even too ambitious for her age.

“My father is a poet and a film buff. I just remember watching esoteric Mongolian cinema with him when I was 11. One of the first films he showed me was ‘Andrei Rublev,’” she laughs.

“The other thing that really informed my filmmaking is that my mum is a mother of three, and my dad was traveling a lot. Every time we went to the movies, we were late. I missed the first 10 minutes of pretty much every movie in my childhood. My concept of cinema was that you were sculpting a narrative out of something you don’t totally understand.”

As a director, she also likes to provide the audience with a “real, visceral experience.” But the situation with indie filmmaking has changed “dramatically.”

“Since Trump came into office, it has become much harder. The first time he was elected, everyone’s integrity was still intact. But America is pretty much run by corporations and they now know how vindictive he is. Everyone has bowed to him in different ways. It’s just survival in a capitalist society.”

She adds: “The things that are getting funded are very safe and appeal to his audience, or ensure they don’t piss his audience off.”

That doesn’t mean that people aren’t trying to be creative, though.

Curry Barker’s horror, produced for just $750,000, became one of the biggest box-office successes of the year.

“Filmmaking is lonelier than ever, probably just because the world is lonelier than ever,” she notes.

“It’s harder than it has ever been in my 20 years of working in this industry. It’s crushing for young folks trying to get started. So many industry players have not been able to make money because the streamers control everything, also because of all these consolidations and mergers. What shocked me when I took my film to Sundance this year was realizing that there are now only three buyers in the American market: HBO, Netflix and Amazon.”

“We put all this pressure on ourselves to get our movie made because we think it’s going to ‘save our souls’. Art won’t save you. But the process [of making it] could,” she states.

“At a time like this, when it’s hard to get things made, it’s important to think about the parts of the process that we love, adore and crave. Making work is really fun. So, so fun! Instead of sitting alone in a room desperately sending your script to everyone in the world, you’re with your friends, writing in community,” she says.

“I’ve never really given this advice before, but find the process you love. Then, hopefully, you’ll eventually get to apply it to cinema, but maybe apply it to your life first.”

Wenger considers standing against Infantino

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Former Arsenal manager Arsene Wenger is considering standing against Gianni Infantino as a candidate in Fifa's presidential election.

For weeks, senior figures in European football have been hoping the Frenchman would run for the job, and he is now giving it serious thought.

Wenger is 77, but some senior figures in the European game have told BBC Sport that they would be happy for him to win the election, then stand down before serving a full four-year term.

Infantino's leadership was plunged into crisis by a backlash caused by his failed plan to sell commercial stakes in the World Cup to private investors.

But he retains support in South America, Africa and Asia, and recently insisted he has the backing of the "vast, vast majority" of world football.

However, that support could be tested if Wenger stands.

Fifa's head of global development would have the backing of Uefa, which has spearheaded opposition to Infantino, and has credibility throughout the footballing world.

Sources close to Wenger say he is yet to decide whether to stand, and still believe Infantino would be favourite to win a fourth and final term in next year's election.

The deadline for candidates to confirm they are standing against Infantino for the election in March is 18 November.

Former Tottenham defender Ramon Vega was the first person to put his name forward in October.

Japan Football Association honorary president Kozo Tashima has also announced his intention to stand.

This month Fifa said "key documents" relating to Infantino's plan to sell stakes in the World Cup would be made available to members of its council, but they will have to travel to its Zurich base, and access will be under "strict protocols".

Last month Uefa gave football's world governing body until 2 November to release details about the controversial Fifa Forward Enterprise (FFE) proposal, and to answer questions relating to its decision-making.

That followed a council meeting at which Infantino apologised for the abandoned sell-off plot but failed to explain how the proposal was devised, asking that questions be submitted in writing.

Amid the fallout over FFE, Wenger, who has traditionally been loyal to Infantino, distanced himself from the aborted plot.

"I was not involved in this strategic plan and first became aware of the project through media reports," Wenger said at the time.

"The decision to withdraw the project was absolutely necessary and beyond question, because I firmly believe in an independent Fifa that serves our game with commitment, transparency and integrity."

Wenger has caused controversy at times in his Fifa role, notably for proposals such as biennial World Cups a "daylight" offside law.

But he also has a global profile, including in Africa, where Infantino recently received a major re-election boost when receiving the backing of all 54 member national associations after promising an increase in funding.

Known as a champion of African players when in charge at Arsenal, Wenger's support of an expanded 48-team World Cup in order to drive development in countries beyond the game's traditional powers has been well received in the continent.

Infantino re-election hopes boosted by Asia support after U-turn

Fifa scraps controversial World Cup investment plan

Ummm. . . Do you know how Medicare works and what options she has (or will have)?

We're in our late 70s and the Part B supplemental plan for each of us is about $500 a month. The Medicare premium is just over $200 and the supplemental plan is about $300 for our age group. Drug plan is extra cost and depends on what meds you take. If you want dental insurance, that is outside of Medicare and we each pay about $50 a month for that. . .

I don't mean to hijack this thread but you may need to reconsider your plan.

Bitcoin (BTC) Capitulation: Retail Holders Are Melting, Dropping BTC Off

📅 2026-11-10  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Bitcoin has been losing wallets on the network since October 16 of this year. With the number of non-empty wallets peaking at 59.99 million that day, the network has already lost roughly 108,700 of them as the price dropped back below $75,000. Smaller holders are clearly running out of patience.

Bitcoin's last bullish hope was a short recovery rally towards the $80,000–$81,000 price threshold in early November. It failed to hold on to that momentum and quickly tumbled towards the $76,000 area, which opened the way towards more losses. Since its October top near $87,000, BTC lost roughly 14% of its value and lost everything it built up since late August.

BTC moves below all major moving averages, with most of them aiming downwards and hinting at the aggravation of the current trend on the market. RSI confirms the thesis, moving around 40 and suggesting a bearish momentum, or a sideways movement at least.

Fueled by the falling price, retail is still fearful: smaller traders are merging or emptying their wallets. The network now holds around 59.88 million non-empty wallets, while exchange supply is down near 1.28 million BTC. The shrinking wallet count, however, isn't something that automatically means Bitcoin is being used less.

But where would BTC finally settle down? Realistically, the closest 'fair' support is the $74,000–$75,000 area, where buyers already stepped in this week. Discussing a breakdown below makes little sense, unfortunately, due to the volatile nature of the market.

But even if BTC holds the $74,000 support, bulls might still stay sidelined. There are little to no signs of retail coming back that can help BTC reach key market levels. Even worse, the market in general isn't favoring bulls, so bracing yourself for a prolonged correction might be the wise choice.

In case bulls somehow wake up, a recovery towards $78,000–$80,000 is the main priority, as it's the area where several moving averages are sitting right now. A falling wallet count with the price holding often acts as a sign of sellers getting exhausted, as smaller investors tend to give up when fear is at its highest.

Whether buyers can defend the $74,000 level or not is the main test for the asset right now. The wallet count keeps shrinking, the price is stuck below the moving averages and a short-lived price bounce here won't change the situation as a whole, unfortunately.

Sources: Mauricio Pochettino to ‘listen’ to Man United plan

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Transfer Correspondent AUTHORITY Former Sky Sports and ESPN FC correspondent who specialises in global recruitment, contract negotiations, and high-velocity transfer breaking news. FOCUS Exclusive reporting, domestic and international transfer markets, and elite-level insights. THE INSIGHT Pete utilises a Global Network of Industry Sources to deliver verified, first-to-market reporting. His “Deep-Lens” analysis connects the dots between boardroom strategy and pitch-level recruitment, providing a level of transfer clarity that goes beyond surface-level rumours.

Mauricio Pochettino would be open to the idea of taking over as Manchester United manager, sources have told Football Insider.

The Argentine is emerging as a potential option to take the reins at Old Trafford after the board decided that Michael Carrick is not the right man to lead them forward.

It is expected that Carrick will be sacked, with an announcement made as early as Tuesday, following a meeting of the club's hierarchy on Monday.

Pochettino has made it clear on several previous occasions that he will always be keen on the idea of returning to the Premier League, despite only recently signing a new contract with the USA national team.

Sources say that the former Tottenham boss is prepared to “listen” if United come to sound him out about the manager’s role, and would consider making the move.

The 54-year-old has been away from English football since the end of his brief stint with Chelsea back in 2024, but could soon be in line for a return.

Pochettino has proven Premier League experience, which has become a big target for the United hierarchy in recent months.

Pochettino is a long-term target for United, but his appointment has consistently eluded them in recent years.

His time at Chelsea didn’t really work out, but he remains widely regarded as one of Tottenham’s best managers in the modern era.

He led his team to second in the Premier League back in 2016-17, which remains their highest league finish since the 1962-63 campaign.

He managed for just shy of 300 games in all competitions, and still boasts a win percentage of over 50 per cent, despite his record being damaged a little by his time at Stamford Bridge.

The United hierarchy still view Pochettino as a genuine top-level boss, even though he has been away from management at club level in the last few years.

Football Insider revealed on Monday that United are planning to do their “due diligence” to explore whether Pochettino could be a viable appointment.

The club hierarchy are continuing to consider Carrick’s future across the international break, and could yet opt to make a change in the dugout.

A move for Pochettino could prove difficult due to his contract with the USA national team, but sources say that United intend to explore whether there is an option to break that deal.

They also have several other bosses on their radar as potential options, including the likes of Eddie Howe and Julian Nagelsmann.

After Maatsen Agreement, Aston Villa Must Agree New Deal with 'Fantastic' Onana

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Aston Villa are said to be closing in on a new contract agreement after striking a 'verbal agreement' with Ian Maatsen, but they must now turn their attention to Amadou Onana.

Unai Emery's men endured a difficult weekend having led 4-2 against Manchester United only to concede twice late on and draw 4-4.It was a sloppy finale from Villa, and Emery was left raging at the full-time whistle. That result followed an away defeat to Barcelona, so it's fair to say Villa go into the international break with a bitter taste in their mouths.

But there is some good news emerging on Monday, with Football Insider reporting that the club have verbally agreed a new contract with Maatsen, who has been a regular this season. The 24-year-old was already tied down until 2030, but Villa are now set to reward him with fresh terms and an extended deal, which is a wise move given his age.

Maatsen has stepped up following the exit of Lucas Digne, and most Villa fans are likely to be in agreement with the new deal when it is finalised.

Villa are taking care of business with Maatsen, and it's believed they are close to announcing a new deal for Emiliano Buendia, whose current deal expires in the summer. Ross Barkley could be in line for a new deal ahead of his deal expiring in the summer, while there will be decisions to be made over John McGinn and Pau Torres, whose deals expire in the summer of 2028, when both players will be North of 30.

Though Pau Torres has already confirmed that he expects to rejoin hometown club Villarreal. He said earlier this year:

"If nothing strange happens, I will complete the two years I have left on my contract and that will be the end of my time at Aston Villa."

"What I would like is for Villarreal not to have to pay anything for me, to arrive as a free agent. It is the club of my life and I do not want them spending money to bring me back. If, when that moment comes, the coach at the club thinks I can provide what is needed, I would be delighted to return. It is something I have in mind and I have spoken about it with the president."

That likely means a new deal for Torres is not on the table, and so Villa should turn their attention to Onana, whose current deal is up in 2029. The midfielder has been vital to Emery's midfield, and his absence this season through a long-term injury has been felt. Onana said of the midfielder back in February: "His potential is still there, and when he is playing, being ready and 100 per cent, his performances are fantastic. He has progressed a lot since being here for a year and a half."

Onana only recently turned 25 years of age, so there is no real concern over the long-term impacts of his injury, and given his importance, Villa don't want to find themselves in a situation where the Belgian has reduced time remaining on his contract and a top club comes and picks him off in Youri Tielemans style, albeit he had a release clause in his deal.

Villa won't be in a rush, given Onana is dealing with a long term issue, but when they look at their list of to-dos on the contract front, the midfielder is likely to be close to the top of it. And while it's a business way of thinking, Onana is likely to appreciate a longer term commitment without any significant wage boost at a time when he is coming back from a serious injury. That length commitment may be sufficient to strike a deal.

Zion Suzuki failed to collect the ball for United's late equaliser.

He’s far more entertaining when you’re laughing at him than with him. But unfortunately, his real talent is his failing ratings.'”

“Sick burn, bro,” Kimmel replied. “You know what? You should enter a rap battle spitting dope rhymes like that.”

He added, “Trump now has interns insulting me. It’s like that sad day when your cat gets to fat to chase mice. This guy says I have a peanut-sized brain. Let me explain something: I know you’re young. That’s impossible. You know what happens when you try to run a full human body with a peanut-sized brain?”

The host continued of Trump’s current demeanor, “I don’t think we’re prepared for the amount of insanity that this drama queen president is about to pump on us. Donald Trump right now is a raccoon in a duffel bag. He’s been cut off at the cankles. And there are two possible paths he can take. The first one is to check out. No more meetings. Even if Don Jr. decides to run for president, his father will say and do anything he can to destroy him. Because he wants to make sure no one but him will ever live in the White House again. Thank you for watching, I’m surprised you’re still up — isn’t it past your jail time?” Trump later called Kimmel a “dope” and “one of the dumbest human beings ever.” Last month, the president described Kimmel as a “loser” during a rally, leading Kimmel to criticize the sparse attendance of the rally itself.

FDA warns of vision loss risk linked to popular GLP-1 drugs like Wegovy, Zepbound

📅 2026-11-10  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The U.S. Food and Drug Administration is requiring new safety warnings on all GLP-1 drugs after finding evidence of a potential increased risk of a rare eye condition, the agency said on Monday.

The condition, which can cause sudden vision loss, known as nonarteritic anterior ischemic optic neuropathy (NAION), is rare, and a causal relationship has not been established.

The FDA is requiring the makers of all GLP-1 medications to add a warning about the potential increased risk of NAION associated with use of these medications, it said.

NAION is a condition that can result in sudden decreased vision, including permanent vision loss. It is caused by decreased blood flow to the front part of the ​optic nerve — the underlying cause is not completely understood, however.

NAION cases have been reported for all currently marketed GLP-1 products, the FDA said.

CNBC has reached out to Ozempic and Wegovy maker Novo Nordisk and Eli Lilly, the company behind Mounjaro, Zepbound and Foundayo, for comment.

The FDA decision follows a review of clinical trials, real-world patient data, and published studies.

An analysis of 126 trials involving more than 134,000 patients found 6.4 cases of NAION per 100,000 patient-years among GLP-1 users, compared with 3.8 among patients receiving other treatments or placebos.

Several observational studies also identified an increased risk, supporting the FDA's concerns.

The FDA's class-wide labeling action is "likely to attract attention but should have limited impact on GLP-1 uptake," according to Citi analysts.

It may temporarily increase patient and investor concern, but the rarity of NAION and substantial weight, glycemic, cardiovascular, renal, and sleep apnea benefits should preserve a favorable benefit-risk profile and limit changes to prescribing the medicines, they added.

While GLP-1 drugs have been shown to lead to benefits beyond weight loss and controlling blood sugar levels, many also carry the FDA's strongest safety warning, known as a boxed warning, over a potential risk of thyroid tumors.

Studies have shown that semaglutide, the active ingredient in Wegovy and Ozempic, and tirzepatide, the active ingredient in Mounjaro, cause thyroid C-cell tumors in rodents. It remains unknown whether the drugs cause these tumors, including a rare form of thyroid cancer, in humans.

OP --- One of the biggest enemies of a decent portfolio is the chase after a perfect one, which is usually futile.

I would consider it an advantage that you are not experienced at trading. If you follow the principles found here you’ll have no need for that. Also, do not feel overwhelmed or less than capable. The process of transitioning from managed to boglehead-type investing is straightforward and there’s a community of support here for every step of the way.
OP, first, welcome to the forum.

Second, I think the 0.40% direct indexing fee desensitized you sufficiently to make you think that a 0.18% fee on your 401k isn’t that bad.

Take some time looking around here. Most of us manage our own funds quite well at much lower expense ratios. You don’t need to know much, and it’s perversely a benefit to know that you don’t know much, because it will help insulate you from bad decisions.

I’m as dumb as a sack of hammers, but the good news is that I know it, and I have successfully managed a substantial heap of assets. Mostly a 3 fund portfolio, plus municipal bonds (live in Massachusetts) and some Berkshire Hathaway, mostly because I dislike dividends (but that’s a personal preference).

ETA: people disagree about direct indexing. I have a relative who does it, but he’s a pro and would never pay anyone 40 basis points to do it for him. I personally don’t care for it, and even if it were free, I’d rather not have a portfolio that looks like a dog’s breakfast.
You may have already done this and not included the info but good planning starts with goals ( what are my needs/wants, annual income from all sources, desires related to Long term care, heirs, etc.).

One old saying is: No one will care more for your money than you will. Self- learning of the benefits of simple, low cost, diverse indexes can have a high ROI.

Once you know your goals, then you can set an asset allocation and place those assets in tax efficient locations and funds. An advisor might be able to help you with that but many are trained in complication and it makes it difficult to see the beauty and profit in simplicity.

There is a sticky note with the best method to share your situation if you desire advice but if you are not yet clear on goals, I would start there.

FWIW, I serve on a couple small endowment investment committees and one started with an advisory. They originally proposed a maddeningly complex investment plan and when we pushed back, they returned with direct investing. My colleagues thought that was a good compromise and I set up a spreadsheet comparing the equity side to VG Total Stock Market. It turned out to be an somewhat expensive compromise and I reported the results quarterly. After a couple of years, we switched to Vanguard and TSM.
I tax loss aggressively. However, I doubt this would be a good option for you.

I think the best starting position for a taxable account is simply to invest in a low-cost US market index. There are a handful of very good passive index US total market ETFs out there with the primary go-to being VTI. VTI has an expense ratio of 0.03%. Compare that to the 0.4% of the proposed direct indexing fund. If you have $500k in your taxable account, that 0.4% fee is $2,000 per year. Assuming no trading friction costs, compare that to holding a S&P 500 ETF or total US stock ETF with a 0.03% expense ratio cost which would be $150. So you would be spending $1,850 extra per year for this direct indexing tax loss harvesting opportunity.

Can you save over that amount each year in taxes through tax loss harvesting? Maybe, maybe not.

TLH opportunities present themselves most often when you are making regular investments into the taxable account. New purchases are much more likely to provide a TLH opportunity than older positions. In retirement, is your excess income going to result in regular new investments in the taxable account?

TLH can capture losses, but they may not provide much in tax-deductible losses that tax year. Capital losses can off-set capital gains. So if you have realized capital gains you would pay less taxes on them. However, you only have realized gains after you have sold the stock for a gain. if you don't sell any stock, you don't have any gains for that year for the losses to off-set. Eventually you will be able to take those losses as they carry over, but you pay the excess $1,850 in fees every year. You can offset up to $3,000 of ordinary income with capital losses each year. If your marginal rate is 22%, you would save $660 in taxes.

However, keep in mind that the $3,000 off-set is not indexed to inflation. So if you have a $9k capital loss, you will be able to take that over three years. However, the real value of that $9k income deduction decreases with inflation. So the total amount deducted from taxable income in real dollars would be only about $8,783. So the real value of that tax loss would be $660 in year 1, $644 in year 2, and $628 in year 3.

My point isn't the actual numbers but to simply illustrate that the value of the tax loss harvesting they are trying to sell you, may not be very high compared to the management fee.

Here is a newsletter issue you may find interesting.
https://thecrunch.co/newsletter/direct- ... arvesting/
Thank you all for your responses! I have plenty to start with and will definitely keep it simple and learn enough to DIY "good enough". thanks
